🔌Menus — Getting Started

🧱Layout & Buttons

Root layout assigns entry IDs to Inventory slots. Ordinary entries are defined under buttons; a Shop menu can also resolve products and buttons from its shop file. Ore and ArcMenu use separate graphical placements.

A complete button menu

Save as menus/common_menus/categories.yml:

Copy

title: '&8Categories'
size: 18
layout:
- '000000000'
- '000A0X000'
buttons:
A:
display-item:
material: WHEAT
name: '&aFarming'
actions:
1:
type: shop_menu
shop: farming
X:
display-item:
material: BARRIER
name: '&cClose'
actions:
1:
type: close

Create the farming shop, reload with /shop reload, then open /shop menu categories. With Inventory selected, A occupies slot 12 and X occupies slot 14, counting slots from zero.

Slot IDs

Each character is one slot. Read left to right, then top to bottom. Use nine slots per row and match size to the total: 9, 18, 27, 36, 45 or 54.

IDs are case-sensitive: A and a differ.

Repeating an ID repeats the same entry in multiple slots.

An undefined ID leaves an empty slot.

0 is an ordinary ID. Defining buttons.'0' fills every matching slot.

For example, this fragment fills nine slots:

Quote numeric-looking IDs in YAML. Decorative Inventory fillers can be hidden from list and graphical presentations as above.

Longer IDs

Enclose a multi-character ID in backticks inside layout. Each enclosed token occupies one slot:

The nine slots are 0, food_buy, food_sell, followed by six 0 slots. The backticks are layout delimiters and are not part of the button keys. Add actions to these display-only example entries to make them interactive.

Entry sources in Shop menus

A template's layout can reference:

Source

Location

Product

Shop items.<id>

Shop-specific ordinary button

Shop buttons.<id>

Reusable ordinary button

Template buttons.<id>

Use distinct IDs for each source. If IDs collide, copied shop entries are checked first where available, followed by shop buttons, shop products and template buttons. The first eligible entry wins.

Ordinary buttons run their actions. Product entries use product trade operations and click bindings such as buy, sell and select-amount. Defining a navigation button with the same display material as a product does not turn it into a trade entry.

Button actions and conditions

Button key

Purpose

display-item

Item appearance; see Display Item Format

actions

Actions when click conditions pass

conditions

Conditions checked when clicking

fail-actions

Actions when click conditions fail

display-conditions

Eligibility to appear — Premium

menu-visibility

Visibility by presentation

dialog.layout, dialog.show-sprite

Native Dialog entry appearance

ui

Ore/ArcMenu appearance and placement

This fragment creates a visible button that checks permission on click:

The vip shop must exist. conditions does not hide the button; use display-conditions when it should disappear. Menu-root conditions controls opening the entire screen. Product buy/sell conditions still validate the transaction separately.

Visibility by presentation

Place this at button or product root:

Unspecified values default to visible. ore and item-action-list fall back to menu-visibility.dialog when not explicitly set. For example, dialog: false hides all three unless an individual mode is explicitly enabled.

Visibility filters an existing entry; it does not create a slot, graphical placement or click action. It can be used independently of dynamic-layout.

Player-dependent layouts — Premium

Enable dynamic-layout: true to evaluate layout placeholders and display conditions for each player. A backtick token can contain ordered alternatives separated by ||:

This row has nine slots. The runtime checks A, then B, selecting the first existing entry allowed in the presentation whose display conditions pass. If neither qualifies, the slot is empty. Put fallback entries last. See Conditional Display for a complete example.

Entry placement by presentation

Presentation

Placement

Inventory

Root layout slot

Form

Entry list; empty Inventory spacing is omitted

Native Dialog

Action/list entries sourced from the root layout

Ore / ArcMenu

Named graphical areas and entry placements

An ordinary graphical button needs an explicit placement:

This uses the selected style's sidebar area. An explicitly placed ordinary button can appear graphically even when absent from root layout. Products can inherit ui.products.placement; product ui.placement overrides that default. No graphical Back/Close/category controls are generated from empty areas.

🔌Menus — Getting Started

⚙️Menu Configuration & Inheritance

Configure shared behavior in config.yml, reusable graphical styles in ui/, and individual screens in menus/. Shop files select a template and can override it for that shop.

File locations

Copy

plugins/UltimateShop/
├── config.yml
├── ui/
│ ├── classic.yml
│ └── bedrock.yml
├── menus/
│ ├── common_menus/
│ ├── shop_menu_templates/
│ ├── buy_more_menus/
│ ├── search_menus/
│ ├── favourite_menus/
│ └── item_sell_menus/
└── shops/

Menu and shop IDs are file names without .yml; custom subfolders do not change those IDs. Style IDs include their relative subfolder: ui/custom/dark.yml is selected as custom/dark.

Global settings and menu settings

This example belongs in config.yml:

The corresponding settings in a menu file have no menu wrapper:

In normal selection, both global and local switches must allow a presentation. Menu dialog.enabled and bedrock.enabled default to true; menu arcmenu.enabled defaults to false. menu.force-ui overrides those switches when enabled. Platform and menu-type requirements still apply. See UI Modes.

Supported native labels and options are resolved from menu dialog.* / bedrock.*, then global menu.dialog.* / menu.bedrock.*. Some integration and resource-generation settings are global only; their presentation pages identify them. Screen descriptions use local dialog.content, bedrock.content or arcmenu.content, falling back to root content.

Shop templates and overrides

A shop selects menus/shop_menu_templates/farming.yml with:

settings.menu-settings belongs in the shop file. It merges into the chosen template before graphical defaults are applied.

Value in the override

Result

A scalar such as title or enabled

Replaces the template value

A nested section

Merges recursively with the template section

A list such as layout or lore

Replaces the entire template list

An omitted key

Preserves the template value

For example, overriding dialog.layout preserves the template's dialog.columns. To replace an Inventory layout, supply all its rows. A shop without settings.menu can define its screen directly in settings.menu-settings.

Shared graphical styles

ui/classic.yml and ui/bedrock.yml provide the bundled graphical defaults. The plugin creates missing bundled style files and preserves existing files. Copy a preset to ui/my-style.yml to maintain your own version.

A style file contains UI fields directly, without a wrapping ui section:

This partial style can be selected with ui.style: my-style; other fields fall back to global graphical defaults. For a complete independent layout, copy a full bundled preset before editing it.

Select the global style in config.yml:

Select a different style for one menu:

Normal graphical inheritance works in this order:

Load the global style; explicit menu.ui values override its defaults.

If a menu selects a different style, overlay that style on the resolved global defaults.

Fill missing menu ui fields from those defaults. Explicit menu fields, including shop overrides, win.

This means a different per-menu style can replace a global accent, while a menu's own ui.theme.accent always wins in normal selection. Nested sections merge recursively; lists are replaced as whole values.

The styles supply graphical layout and appearance for Ore/ArcMenu. ui.style: bedrock changes graphics; it does not turn a Java screen into a Bedrock Form.

Force a presentation and style

In config.yml:

type selects the presentation globally. A nonempty style also overrides menu-local graphical values wherever the resolved forced defaults provide that field. Local fields absent from those defaults remain. Entry placements and product-level overrides still need to fit the chosen layout.

Leave style: '' to force only the presentation and retain normal style selection. Both forced presentation and forced style are ignored when enabled: false. Supported types and client behavior are listed in UI Modes.

Product and button settings

Purpose

Location

Ordinary button item and actions

Menu buttons.<id>.display-item/actions

Product item, prices and trade rules

Shop items.<id>

Presentation visibility

Button/product menu-visibility.<presentation>

Graphical product placement

Menu ui.products.placement, overridden by product ui.placement

Ordinary graphical button placement

Button ui.placement

Graphical product price and actions

Product ui.price / ui.actions, then owning shop menu ui.products.*, then global menu.ui.products.*

Graphical card styling

Menu ui.products.card

Form icon and supplemental line

Active display-item.bedrock.*

Form direct product action

Product bedrock.click-event

A product displayed in Search/Favourite retains its source product settings and trade rules. Graphical action/price lookup uses the owning shop menu for its menu-level defaults.

Scope by menu type

Common and Shop support Ore and ArcMenu defaults. Search and Favourite inherit ArcMenu defaults but exclude Ore template and card-lore defaults. Item Sell and Buy More do not inherit graphical menu.ui settings. Buy More reads its own dialog.enabled for native quantity input; its source shop supplies Form/Dialog quantity labels. Sell All uses only its dedicated menu.sell-all screen configuration.

Reload with /shop reload after editing menus, styles or selectors. Ore layout and texture changes also require rebuilding and distributing its resource pack.

🔌Menus — Getting Started

📚Menu System

Menus define the screens players open. A menu's type determines its content and controls; its presentation determines whether players see an Inventory, Form, Dialog, Ore or ArcMenu screen. Shop products, prices and trade conditions stay in the shop configuration.

All configuration paths in this section are relative to plugins/UltimateShop/. Menu and shop IDs are file names without .yml. Keep IDs unique so commands and actions resolve to the intended screen.

Create your first menu

Save this complete example as menus/common_menus/hello.yml:

Copy

title: '&8Hello'
size: 9
layout:
- '0000X0000'
dialog:
enabled: false
bedrock:
enabled: false
arcmenu:
enabled: false
buttons:
X:
display-item:
material: BARRIER
name: '&cClose'
actions:
1:
type: close

Run /shop reload, then /shop menu hello. This creates a one-row Inventory with a Close button in its fifth slot. The example uses the normal presentation switches; an enabled global menu.force-ui takes priority over them.

Each layout character represents a slot. X selects buttons.X; undefined 0 entries leave empty slots. Learn longer IDs, click actions and visibility in Layout & Buttons.

Choose a menu type

Type

Configuration

Player flow

Common

menus/common_menus/<id>.yml

Navigation and custom actions

Shop

shops/<id>.yml and menus/shop_menu_templates/<template>.yml

Browse and trade shop products

Buy More

menus/buy_more_menus/<id>.yml

Select a quantity for an existing product

Search — Premium

menus/search_menus/<id>.yml

Find products by item or name

Favourite — Premium

menus/favourite_menus/<id>.yml

Save products and edit their order

Item Sell

menus/item_sell_menus/<id>.yml

Deposit items, preview value and confirm

Sell All

config.yml → menu.sell-all

Deposit items and sell on close

Use /shop menu <id> for Common, Shop, Search, Favourite and Item Sell. Buy More needs a selected product; Sell All opens with /shop sellall or its menu action.

Choose a presentation

Inventory uses the root size and layout. Premium adds Bedrock Form, native Java Dialog, graphical Ore and ArcMenu. Availability depends on the menu type, server platform and installed integrations. See UI Modes for the support table and selection order.

Graphical styles live in ui/. Select a shared style with global menu.ui.style or a menu's ui.style; configure coordinates with ui.layout and entry ui.placement. The bedrock style is a graphical preset and is separate from Bedrock Form.

Continue configuring

Configuration & Inheritance: files, templates, styles and override priority.

Layout & Buttons: slot IDs, actions, conditions and visibility.

Graphical UI Layout — Premium: areas, cards, pagination and themes.

Conditional Display — Premium: player-dependent entries and alternatives.

Display Item Add Lore: prices, limits and presentation-specific instructions.

Configuration Compatibility: current keys and migration examples.

Shared formats: Display Item, Actions and Conditions.

🔌Menus — Getting Started

📐Graphical UI Layout - Premium

Ore and ArcMenu share graphical styles, a logical canvas, named areas and entry placements. These settings position graphical controls; Inventory uses root layout, and native Form/Dialog use their own controls.

Common/Shop support both graphical renderers. Search/Favourite support ArcMenu; Buy More and physical selling screens do not use this canvas. See UI Modes.

Start with a shared style

Select global menu.ui.style in config.yml, or menu-root ui.style. Bundled classic uses left navigation; bedrock uses a top navigation bar. Both provide named sidebar, content and footer areas. Style files live under plugins/UltimateShop/ui/ and contain UI fields directly, without a wrapping ui section.

This complete Common menu uses the inherited canvas:

Copy

title: '&8Categories'
size: 9
layout:
- '0000A0000'
dialog:
enabled: true
layout: ore
arcmenu:
enabled: false
ui:
style: classic
buttons:
A:
display-item:
material: WHEAT
name: '&aFarming'
actions:
1:
type: shop_menu
shop: farming
ui:
appearance: button
placement:
area: sidebar
order: 0

Create the target shop. For Ore, enable global Dialog, reload and generate/distribute its pack. For ArcMenu, enable the global/menu ArcMenu switches with a compatible integration. The menu's root Inventory layout does not select graphical coordinates.

For shop product cards, the inherited placement is normally:

Configuration scopes

Scope

Configuration

Shared style file

ui/<style>.yml: layout, products, theme, text, renderers

Global graphical defaults

config.yml → menu.ui

Per-menu overrides

Menu root ui; for a shop, also settings.menu-settings.ui

Individual product actions/price

Product root ui.actions, ui.price

Individual entry placement

Product/button root ui.placement

Ordinary entry appearance

Entry ui.appearance, ui.font-size, ui.text-align

In normal selection, explicit menu fields win over selected defaults. A different per-menu style overlays the global defaults; missing fields are filled recursively. Forced style selection can replace local defaults. See Configuration & Inheritance.

Graphical product action/price lookup is product → owning shop menu → global defaults. Card appearance is menu-wide. Native Form price lines use bedrock.price-extra-line, independently of graphical prices.

Canvas and areas

This menu fragment shows a complete classic-style canvas definition:

Style sections merge recursively. Editing header.title does not remove other named headers inherited from a style; set unwanted header entries to enabled: false.

Path relative to ui.layout

Purpose

canvas.width, canvas.height

Logical dimensions: width 144–900, height 144–540

header.<id>.enabled/text

Named header visibility and text

header.<id>.x/y/width/height

Header bounds; default height 27

header.<id>.align

left, center or right

header.<id>.font-size, bold

ArcMenu text scale and bold styling

areas.<id>.type

grid, stack or free

areas.<id>.x/y/width/height

Area bounds on the canvas

areas.<id>.columns/rows

Grid dimensions: columns 1–36, rows 1–60

areas.<id>.cell-height

Grid cell height; width derives from area width/columns/gaps

areas.<id>.direction

Stack direction: vertical (default) or horizontal

areas.<id>.item-width

Horizontal stack entry width; default 108

areas.<id>.item-height

Stack entry height; default 36

areas.<id>.gap

Fallback for horizontal and vertical gaps

areas.<id>.gap-x/gap-y

Individual gap overrides

areas.<id>.background-enabled/background-padding

ArcMenu area backdrop and padding

pagination.enabled

Graphical page controls

pagination.previous/indicator/next

Each supports enabled, x, y, width, height

Headers can replace {title} and {content} and use normal language/PlaceholderAPI text. Ore description comes from dialog.content, ArcMenu from arcmenu.content, then local root content.

Area behavior and pagination

Grid fills cells in order, reserving explicitly assigned row/column cells first. A 3 × 2 area holds six entries per page. Additional grid entries produce further pages; row/column indices start at zero, and rows beyond the first page identify later pages. Keep columns within the configured grid width.

Stack lays out entries in placement.order, vertically or horizontally. It does not create extra pages; all entries must fit. The bundled Bedrock style's horizontal sidebar holds five navigation buttons and remains visible while grid pages change.

Free uses offsets inside the area's bounds. Supply both placement.x and placement.y. Default free-entry size is 108 × 27; larger entries still need to fit the area.

Canvas height, all element Y coordinates/heights, and vertical gaps must be multiples of 9. Bounds must stay inside their parent. Rectangles require width at least 24 and height at least 9; non-free cells require height at least 27. Every appearance: card requires height at least 99. Visible entries must not overlap each other, headers or active pagination controls.

Search/Favourite root result-slot counts limit source entries before graphical arrangement. A larger grid or more graphical pages does not expand that source capacity.

Entry placements

The target Search menu must exist and requires Premium. The Close button is positioned relative to the footer's origin. Both controls can appear graphically without root layout markers.

Entry key

Purpose

ui.placement.enabled

Default true when placement exists; false disables graphical placement

ui.placement.area

Named area ID

ui.placement.order

Sort priority; defaults to source slot

ui.placement.row/column

Explicit grid cell; a configured row enables explicit-cell placement

ui.placement.x/y

Free-area offsets

ui.placement.width/height

Entry dimension overrides

ui.appearance

button or card; products default to cards

ui.font-size

Ordinary ArcMenu button font scale

ui.text-align

Ordinary graphical button text alignment

menu-visibility.ore/arcmenu

Presentation visibility at entry root

Ordinary entries need their own placement; products inherit ui.products.placement and merge their own overrides over it. appearance: card can also create category/navigation cards. Neither an empty sidebar nor an action automatically generates a navigation control.

Product cards, prices and actions

Path relative to ui.products

Purpose

card.icon-size, padding

Item icon size and content padding

card.name-size/price-size

ArcMenu product name/price font scales

card.button-font-size/button-height

ArcMenu operation button text and height

card.buttons.inset-x/inset-bottom/gap

ArcMenu operation button placement

card.lore-lines

Maximum Ore product body lore lines

card.description-lines

Maximum Ore category description lines; ArcMenu fits descriptions to available space

price.enabled

Compact product price line

price.formats.both/only-buy/only-sell

Format for the product's available trade directions

actions.primary

Ordered preferred IDs; at most two available primary operations

actions.extra

auto for remaining operations, an explicit list, or [] for none

actions.labels.<id>

Operation label; supports {item-name} and normal text placeholders

Operation IDs come from product click bindings and custom menu.click-event-actions. Listing buy does not create a missing binding. Prices, product options and custom buy-only/sell-only restrictions filter available actions.

Products can override ui.price and ui.actions at product root; card styling remains menu-wide. Compact price text represents one trade unit and uses shared dynamic price/multiplier calculation. Information/details use their applicable quantities. Original lore and applicable generated lore remain available to information/tooltips. Use lore presentation conditions for different instructions.

Theme and text

Quote colors in YAML. ArcMenu accepts #RRGGBB or #AARRGGBB; Ore's generated RGB textures discard alpha.

Path relative to ui.theme

Purpose

background-enabled, background

ArcMenu window backdrop

sidebar, card, border, accent, text

Shared panel/card/accent colors and ArcMenu base text

button.background/hover/border/text-align

Ordinary graphical controls

buy.background/hover, sell.background/hover

Trade controls

title.background-enabled/background/decoration-enabled

Independent ArcMenu title background and decoration

decorations.enabled/style/color/corner-color

ArcMenu frame/corner styling

decorations.thickness/corner-thickness/corner-length/inset

Decoration dimensions

decorations.dividers-enabled

ArcMenu header accent

bevel.enabled/shadow/top/left/right/bottom

ArcMenu beveled control defaults

tooltip.border/background

ArcMenu hover panel colors

details.background, more-menu.background

ArcMenu details and popup colors

ArcMenu control-specific theme sections such as buy, sell, quantity, pagination and cancel can supply their own text and bevel colors. Bevel colors are composited against their control's base color.

A title background can be enabled separately from the window backdrop:

Decoration style accepts frame, corners, both or none. none adds no outer frame while allowing the header accent. Supported dimensions are thickness 0.5–6, corner thickness 0.5–8, corner length 0–80, inset 0–30. Title backgrounds/decorations follow the configured title-header bounds.

Graphical labels live in ui.text: cancel, no-extra, amount-input, select-product and favourite-edit.forward/backward/remove. Language references can localize them.

Renderer-specific settings

Ore texture templates belong in ui.renderers.ore.templates; ArcMenu interaction, animation and tooltip options belong in ui.renderers.arcmenu. ArcMenu refresh update-interval is global only. Shared geometry does not promise identical font rendering or card bodies across the two renderers.

Ore · ArcMenu · Configuration Compatibility

🧭Menu Types

🔲Common Menus

Common menus display configured buttons for navigation and custom actions. Save them in menus/common_menus/ and open them with /shop menu <id> or an open_menu action.

Create a navigation menu

Save as menus/common_menus/main.yml:

Copy

title: '&8Main Menu'
size: 9
layout:
- '000A0X000'
dialog:
enabled: false
bedrock:
enabled: false
arcmenu:
enabled: false
buttons:
A:
display-item:
material: WHEAT
name: '&aFarming'
actions:
1:
type: shop_menu
shop: farming
X:
display-item:
material: BARRIER
name: '&cClose'
actions:
1:
type: close

Create the farming shop, run /shop reload, then /shop menu main. The example selects Inventory in normal selection; global menu.force-ui can override the switches.

Use type: shop_menu with shop to open a shop. Use type: open_menu with menu for another Common, Search, Favourite or Item Sell menu. A Common menu does not automatically populate shop products.

Common menu keys

Key

Purpose

title

Screen title

content

Shared description fallback

size, layout

Inventory size and entry IDs

buttons

Ordinary display items, actions and conditions

conditions

Conditions for opening the menu

open-actions, close-actions

Actions for menu lifecycle events

dynamic-layout

Per-player layout evaluation — Premium

dialog, bedrock, arcmenu

Presentation switches and supported native options

ui

Ore/ArcMenu style, layout and drawing settings

Button click conditions belong in buttons.<id>.conditions; button display conditions belong in buttons.<id>.display-conditions. Root conditions applies to the whole menu. See Layout & Buttons.

Use another presentation — Premium

To use native Dialog, replace the example's Dialog switch with:

Also enable global menu.dialog.enabled. For Ore, select dialog.layout: ore, configure graphical placements and distribute its generated pack. For ArcMenu, enable the global/menu ArcMenu switches and configure graphical placements with a compatible integration. Bedrock Form uses the global/menu Bedrock switches.

UI Modes explains priority, forced selection and supported clients. Graphical UI Layout explains styles and areas.

Custom command — Premium

Add this at menu root to register a command for the menu:

The command opens this menu for the player. Choose a name that does not conflict with an existing command.

Inventory refresh and title updates

Global menu.menu-update.circle-update refreshes item displays every second; click-update refreshes after clicks. Premium title updates use menu.title-update.enabled/circle-update/click-update and require PacketEvents. Full settings are listed in Inventory UI.

Typed files in the Common folder

The dedicated Search, Favourite and Item Sell folders classify their files automatically. Existing Common-folder files can declare root menu-type: search, favourite (alias favorite) or item-sell (alias item_sell). Search/Favourite IDs can also be classified by their global legacy menu lists.

Prefer dedicated folders for new typed menus. Their specialized controls and presentation support follow their declared type, even when stored in common_menus.

📍Placeholders

🔧Built-in Placeholders

The {random-last-reset_<ID>} placeholder displays the last time a random placeholder value was reset. It returns the configured placeholder.refresh.never text before the first reset.

By default, placeholder displayed in GUI will only refreshed when player open the menu or click the button. If you want to auto update placeholder value, you need enable menu.shop.update option in config.yml file. Remeber: this can even cost x20 plugin performance than normal when open the GUI. This is NOT a good choice to enable this.

If your shop ID includes _ symbol, you can replace them to - symbol to avoid plugin may not parse them correctly.

If the player has not purchased this item or the buy time has been reset, it will return last buy time.

PlaceholderAPI Support

{random_<ID>}

Query random placeholder's first picked element.
For more info about random placeholder, please view Randoms Placeholders page.

Anywhere in plugin
PREMIUM

{random_<ID>;;<Number>}

Quert random placeholder specife number of picked element, if this number of picked element does not exist, we will quert the last picked element. You can set max amount of picked element by element-amount option in random placeholder.

Anywhere in plugin
PREMIUM

{random-times_<ID>}

View random placeholder refresh time.

Anywhere in plugin
PREMIUM

{random-next_<ID>}

View countdown time for random placeholder refresh time. If the refresh time is not generated, we will return a string set in config.yml.

Anywhere in plugin
PREMIUM

{conditional_<ID>}

Use conditional placeholder.
For more info, please view Conditional Placeholders page.

Anywhere in plugin
PREMIUM

{custom_<ID>}

Use custom placeholder.
For more info, please view Custom Placeholders page.

Anywhere in plugin
PREMIUM

{compare_
<number1>_<number2}

Compare 2 numbers. Result format can be changed in config.yml file.

Anywhere in plugin
PREMIUM

{cron_"<cronStr>"}

Print Cron format result.
Don't miss out the " symbol! Should use Quartz format.

Anywhere in plugin

{math_<mathStr>}

Calculate the math string you put. Like {math_10+50} will print 60.
Require you enable math.enabled option in config.yml file.
You can set result scale at placeholder.math.scale option.

Anywhere in plugin

Sell chest hologram placeholders

The sell chest hologram also supports {id}, {owner}, {usage}, {multiplier}, and {price}. See the Sell Chest page for the value and behavior of each placeholder.

Use built-in placeholder in amount option requires you change this option in config.yml file from

to

PlaceholderAPI Support

All built-in placeholders above that has PlaceholderAPI Support tag can be used in PlaceholderAPI expansion:

Use %ultimateshop_<shopID>_<productID>_<builtInPlaceholder>% to display built-in placeholder outsite of the plugin!

For example:

%ultimateshop_example_A_{buy-limit-player}%

Start from 2.5.6 version, you don't need put {} symbol into builtInPlaceholder arg, new format example, if you want to use this placeholderapi in our plugin, you have to use this new format becuase we will auto parse built-in placeholder into the value, this new format can avoid this:

%ultimateshop_example_A_buy-limit-player%

For random, random times, random last reset, random next, compare and conditional placeholder, you don't need specife the shop and the product, just put the placeholder after ultimateshop. For example:

%ultimateshop_{random-times_rotate}%

This don't support remove {} symbol.

New Line Symbol

Use ;; symbol if you want to start a new line, this is very useful for some people want to display price in multi lines.

Built-in Placeholders List

📍Placeholders

⛓️Conditional Placeholder - Premium

We added {conditional} built-in placeholder in plugin.

Config

All conditional placeholder configs are stored in placeholders/conditional_placeholders folder. The file name is it's ID, for example: buy.yml means it's ID is buy. An example of it's config is like below:

Copy

mode: MAX

value:
default: 1
vip: 1.5
mvp: 2

conditions:
vip:
1:
type: permission
permission: 'group.vip'
mvp:
1:
type: permission
permission: 'group.mvp'

mode: The type of conditional placeholder.

DEFAULT: The basic mode checks whether the player meets the corresponding conditions from top to bottom, and if they do, the corresponding value will be immediately returned.

MAX: Require all values of the placeholder to be numbers, and the maximum value that meets the condition will be returned.

MIN: Similar to MAX, but min value will be returned.

conditions: The condition of each value. Each condition id section should use Condition Format.

value: The value of each condition. A option called default is the value returned that if no condition meet.

Use Placeholder

Use {conditional_<ID>} placeholder to display it's value. For more info, please view Placeholders page. For example: {conditional_buy}

Example: Conditional Product

Create a new conditional placeholder like this:

Make sure your shop includes product with ID A, B, C.

Open your shop's menu configs, find layout option:

Replace yourPlaceholderName to the file name of the conditional placeholder you used.

Next🗯️Custom Placeholder - Premium

Example: Conditional Product

Copy

mode: DEFAULT

value:
default: A # Product ID
vip: B # Product ID
mvp: C # Product ID

conditions:
vip:
1:
type: permission
permission: 'group.vip'
mvp:
1:
type: permission
permission: 'group.mvp'

Copy

dynamic-layout: true

layout:
- '000000000'
- '000`{conditional_yourPlaceholderName}``{conditional_yourPlaceholderName}``{conditional_yourPlaceholderName}`000'
- '000000000'
- 'a0003000b'

📍Placeholders

🗯️Custom Placeholder - Premium

This feature was added in version 4.5.26, and still in BETA test.

The main purpose of a custom placeholder is to record desired information, such as the quantity of same of material sold, or the earning. Simply modify the placeholder value using commands in the appropriate places to achieve the desired effect.

Config

All custom placeholder configs are stored in placeholders/custom_placeholders folder. The file name is it's ID, for example: example.yml means it's ID is example. An example of it's config is like below:

Copy

type: number
default-value: "1"
per-player-value: true

type: Support value: default or number. If you only want to record numeric data, then set this to number; otherwise, set it to default.

default-value: If no value is set for this placeholder, the default value set here will be used.

per-player-value: If set to true, each player has an independent placeholder value; otherwise, all players share the same placeholder value.

Use Placeholder

Use {custom_<ID>} placeholder to display it's value. For more info, please view Placeholders page. For example: {custom_example}

Set Placeholder Value

You need to set the value of the custom placeholder by using commands.

/shop setcustomplaceholder <ID> <value> [playerName]
If per-player-result is set to true, then playerName will be a required parameter; otherwise, the playerName parameter should not be entered.

/shop addcustomplaceholder <ID> <value> [playerName]
Only work number type of custom placeholder. It will be added to the current value of the placeholder. For example, if the current value is 1 and the value parameter entered here is 5, then the final placeholder value will be replaced with 6.

Previous⛓️Conditional Placeholder - Premium

Next🔖Example: Discount

Last updated 1 month ago

Set Placeholder Value

📍Placeholders

🔖Example: Discount

Create Conditional Placeholder

Use conditional placeholder with MAX or MIN mode, discount feature can be easily implemented.

Here we created 2 conditional placeholder for buy and sell.

Copy

mode: MAX

value:
default: 1
vip: 1.5
mvp: 2

conditions:
vip:
1:
type: permission
permission: 'group.vip'
mvp:
1:
type: permission
permission: 'group.mvp'

Use the placeholder in product configs

5 is base price, then add {conditional_buy} or {conditional_sell} before it.

If player meet vip condition, the {condition_buy} will return 0.5, so he will only need pay 0.5*4 which means only 2.5 coins!

Auto Apply Discount

Start from 2.3.2, you can auto apply conditional placeholder to all prices! Just configure it in config.yml file. This can helps you auto add discount for each products.

It is recommend you disable this feature for dynamic price, if you want to enable it for dynamic price too, just change black-dynamic-price option to false here!

If you want to disable this feature for specified shops, use black-shops option.

Previous🗯️Custom Placeholder - Premium

Next🔄Dynamic Price

default: 1

mvp: 0.3

conditions:
vip:
1:
type: permission

mvp:
1:

Copy

items:
A:
price-mode: CLASSIC_ALL
product-mode: CLASSIC_ALL
products:
1:
material: sea_lantern
lore:
- '%player_health%'
buy-prices:
1:
economy-plugin: Vault
amount: '{conditional_buy} * 5' # <--- Changed line
start-apply: 0
placeholder: '{amount} Coins'
sell-prices:
1:
economy-plugin: Vault
amount: '{conditional_sell} * 5' # <--- Changed line
start-apply: 0
placeholder: '{amount} Coins'

Copy

placeholder:
auto-settings:
add-conditional-in-all-price-amount:
enabled: false
buy-placeholder: buy
sell-placeholder: sell
black-dynamic-price: true
black-shops:
- 'example'

📍Placeholders

🎲Random Placeholder - Premium

We added {random} built-in placeholder in premium version. You can use random placeholder at the options that support the use of placeholders to randomly select the value. For example, random price amount, random reset time, random stock, etc.

Config

All random placeholder configs are stored in placeholders/random_placeholders folder. The file name is it's ID, for example: rotate.yml means it's ID is rotate. An example of it's config is like below:

Copy

reset-mode: TIMED
reset-time: '00:00:00'
per-player-element: true
element-sort: true
element-amount: 5
elements:
A:
rate: 1
conditions:
1:
type: permission
permission: 'test.permission'
B:
rate: 5
C:
rate: 2
D:
rate: 5
E:
rate: 2
F:
rate: 15
G:
rate: 7

per-player-element: If set to false, all players will use the same value output by random placeholder. For example, player 1 will get A, and player 2 will get the same value, even if the player reaches 100 million, the value will be the same. If set to true, the values output by each player will be different from each other, and conditions can be set for elements. If an element does not meet the conditions, the player will never be able to extract it.

This option is NOT recommended to be changed after enabling this random placeholder, as it may cause the plugin to detect errors (such as a per player random placeholder appearing in the global database) and output a prompt in the console that cannot be blocked.
The random placeholder of per player cannot be applied to global server scenarios, and vice versa. Random placeholders without per player enabled cannot be applied to per player scenarios. For example, when using the resetrandomplaceholder command, the per player's random placeholder must enter the player name in the command parameters, while the per player's random placeholder cannot enter the player name in the command parameters, otherwise the plugin will prompt an error.

element-sort: If set to false, the result of the random placeholder will be out of order. For example, if you set elements to A, B, C, D, E and the randomly generated result is B, D, the result of the random placeholder may be B, D, or D, B. However, if set to true, the result will be output strictly in the order of elements, and the output result can only be B, D. (Added in 3.12.2)

reset-mode/reset-time: Please view below to know.

element-amount: The amount of the element will picked in this placeholder. (Added in 3.1.0)

elements: The random element what placeholder will picked.

There are two ways to express elements.

The first is to write all elements in the form of a list, where the rate of each element is equal and without any conditions. Support use ~ symbol means pick random number, for example, 5~100 means pick one random number from 5 to 100.

The second is to use the form of subsections, where each element has options for rate and conditions to fill in. The conditions option is not required and only support when per-player-element option being set to true. (Added in 3.12.0)

Use Placeholder

Use {random_<ID>;;<Number>} placeholder to display it's value, like {random_daily;;2} will query daily random placeholder's second element picked. For more info, please view Placeholders page. For example of this placeholder usage, please view Daily Shop page.

Use {random-times_<ID>} placeholder to display the reset time of the placeholder, like {random-times_daily}.

Use {random-last-reset_<ID>} placeholder to display the last time its value was reset, like {random-last-reset_daily}. It displays Never until the first reset occurs.

Reset Placeholder

You can reset placeholder by setting reset-mode and reset-time option or use /shop resetrandomplaceholder command.

Reset Mode

Supports below reset mode:

NEVER

ONCE: Each time the placeholer is used, it will reset and is not able to use in the price, as the price seen by the player opening the shop and the actual transaction result are calculated twice, so you cannot achieve price synchronization which means price player seen in shop will not same as the price player actually cost.

TIMER: It will reset after the time you specify, for example, after 5 hours.

TIMED: It will be reset at the corresponding time, such as 8:15 pm.

CUSTOM: Directly enter the reset time in reset time, and the plugin will not perform any calculations. Recommend obtain reset time through the Placeholder API results. You need set time format at time-format option to helps us know how does your PlaceholderAPI results be like.

We first generate the reset time after the random placeholder is used. CUSTOM targets are re-evaluated when the placeholder or its refresh time is queried, and the pooled task is updated when the target changes.

Do NOT use COOLDOWN_TIMER, COOLDOWN_TIMED, or COOLDOWN_CUSTOM reset mode here; they are not supported by random placeholders.

Reset Time

Different reset modes require different values to be filled in here. Supports placeholders, the placeholder used here must be on the server side, which means that all players receive the same value.

NEVER

Don't need anything here.

TIMER

You can enter 3 to 5 numbers here, separated by a : symbol between each number. For example: 15:00:00.

Each number from right to left represents:

Seconds

Minutes

Hours

Days

Months

In this example, represents 15 hours later. Which means: if now time is 2023-09-04 12:00:00. Will reset after 15 hours, which means 2023-09-05 03:00:00.

TIMED

The composition of TIMED and TIMER is almost identical, but the first three digits from the right-hand side represent the time of day. Let's also take 15:00:00 as an example:

If now time is 2023-09-04 12:00:00, will reset at 2023-09-04 15:00:00.

This is the result obtained with days set to 0. If you set it to 1, we will add another day, and that's it.

If you want to do a daily shop, days should be set to 0, and if you want to do a weekly shop, days should be set to 6. Because you need to reset the number of times on the last day, not on the second day after the last day, right?

This type of reset mode also supports set multi reset time, each reset time use ;; to splite, we will pick up the earliest reset time. For example:

In this example, this random placeholder will reset at 19:00 and 20:00 every day.

CUSTOM

You only need to enter a Placeholder API placeholder here, and the result of the placeholder must include the complete year, month, day, hour, minute, and second. You also need to enter its format in the time-format option. The same CUSTOM target executes only once; lastResetTime is persisted so it will not repeat after a restart.

Cron Reset (Weekly Reset/Monthly Reset)

You can use Cron format in reset time.

Set reset mode to CUSTOM.

Use {cron_"<Cron Expression>"} built-in placeholder in reset time. Don't miss out the " symbol. The <Cron Expression> should use Quartz format.

For example:

You can obtain the Cron expression you want by asking ChatGPT or use this tool. For example, the Cron expression in this example means to reset at 0:00 every Thursday. We do not provide any help related to how to write Cron expression.

You MUST make sure that time format of the result of Cron placeholder (set it in config.yml file) and the time-format you set here is same. By default, they are same.

Testing

You can know about the reset time of the random placeholder by using the command /shop getplaceholdvalue {random times_<placeholdID>}. And by using /shop getplaceplaceervalue {random_<placeholdID>} to know the current value of the random placeholder, you can compare whether the value before reset is the same after reset by this way.

Example: Random Price

Create new random placeholder

In this example, we create a new random placeholder config called price.yml at random_placeholder folder.

Set dynamic value in your product configs

Use {random_price} placeholder at any product's price config. Like I add this placeholder in this product:

Reset Mode

Reset Time

NEVER

TIMER

Example: Random Price

Create new random placeholder

Set dynamic value in your product configs

Copy

elements:
- 'A'
- 'B'
- 'C'

Copy

elements:
# Random number from 5 to 100.
- '5~100'

Copy

per-player-element: true # <--- Only support use condition when this option to true
elements:
A:
rate: 1
conditions:
1:
type: permission
permission: 'test.permission'
B:
rate: 5
C:
rate: 2

Copy

reset-mode: 'TIMED'
reset-time: '20:00:00;;19:00:00'

Copy

reset-mode: 'CUSTOM'
reset-time: '{cron_"0 0 0 ? * 5}'
time-format: 'yyyy-MM-dd HH:mm:ss'

Copy

reset-mode: TIMED
reset-time: '00:00:00'
element-sort: true
element-amount: 50
elements:
# Random number from 5 to 100.
- '5~100'
- '4~40'
- '53~530'
- '32~140'
- '55~140'

Copy

items:
A:
price-mode: CLASSIC_ALL
product-mode: CLASSIC_ALL
products:
1:
material: coal
amount: 1
buy-prices:
1:
economy-plugin: Vault
amount: '{random_price;;1}' # <--- Changed line
placeholder: '&6{amount} Coins'
start-apply: 0

🛍️Shops

⚡Example: Common Usage

Copy

settings:
menu: 'example-shop-menu'
buy-more: true
shop-name: 'Blocks Shop'
hide-message: false

items:
A:
# ...
B:
# ...
C:
# ...

Product A - VIP Limits

This example has sell limits, which means player can only sell this product X times every day.

Sell limits can help your server has "economy balance", even player has 10000 rotten flesh, he still can only sell 64x in this example.

In this example, VIP players will have 50% more sell limits, which can help your server has more supporter.

In this example, sell limits will reset at local time '0:00:00', which means midnight.

In this example, we set price and product mode has CLASSIC_ prefix, this will save server performance, you should use this when you didn't set start-apply option to other value in prices section.

Product B - Experience Price

Product A only brought an item from vanilla, let's try to obtain an item from a third-party plugin! Meanwhile, using Vault as an economy is too boring! Let's try something different, such as vanilla's experience points!

To get how to write hook-plugin option and hook-item option, please read Item Format page.

To get how to write economy-type option or economy-plugin option, please read Economy Format page.

Product C - Command Product

Sometimes, we do not support the third-party plugin items you are using, so you can solve this by creating a command shop!

You can first enter the command /shop generateitemformat to generate an Item Format and fill it into the display-item option. This way, even if you haven't set any items, the plugin can still display the item in the store by reading the item in the display-item option.

This product didn't set sell-prices option, so it can not be sold.

After purchase, we will execute buy-actions which includes execute the command you set. For more info about Actions, please read Action Format page.

🛍️Shops

🏆Example: Daily Rewards

Rewards is very similar to Shops, but rewards don't have any price, and usually, it needs to be coordinated with the menu system.

Example for Daily Rewards

Create new shop config at /shops/ folder, then create new product like this:

Copy

A:
price-mode: CLASSIC_ALL
product-mode: CLASSIC_ALL
products:
1:
material: DIAMOND
amount: 16
2:
material: IRON_INGOT
amount: 64
3:
economy-plugin: Vault
amount: 1500
buy-prices:
1:
economy-type: exp
amount: 0
placeholder: 'Free'
buy-limits:
default: '1'
buy-times-reset-mode: 'TIMED'
buy-times-reset-time: '00:00:00'

In this example, players can purchase this "product" once each day, and because the price is free, so we can consider it as "reward".

If you want to make VIP players have bonus 64x bread, then just use our conditions system, like this:

If you also want to VIP players have 50% chance to get bonus 1x diamond sword, then we need create a new random placeholder at config.yml:

Then also use condition system:

🛍️Shops

📅Example: Daily Shop/Rotating Shop

This example will only work for PREMIUM version of UltimateShop!
This example is using random placeholder to display different product in one shop, you can also use conditinal placeholder to make different condition display different product, for example of it, please view here.

Create random placeholder

We need to create a random placeholder.

Save the random placeholder as placeholders/random_placeholders/daily.yml.

The placeholder draws five product IDs from elements each day. The menu uses the first three results. Each ID in elements must match a key under items in the shop file.

Please view Random Placeholder page for more info about random placeholder.

Copy

reset-mode: TIMED
reset-time: '00:00:00'
element-amount: 5
per-player-element: false
element-sort: true
elements:
- 'redstone'
- 'iron_ingot'
- 'gold_ingot'
- 'copper_ingot'
- 'diamond'
- 'lapis_lazuli'
- 'emerald'

Configure Shop

The various options used in this example are detailed on the Shops page. If you are unsure of the purpose of each option, please refer to that article.

Add products A through G; their IDs must match the placeholder elements.

Configure Menu

The various options used in this example are detailed on the Menus page. If you are unsure of the purpose of each option, please refer to that article.

Each product slot tries the random placeholder first. If that product cannot appear in the slot, P shows the product in the corresponding position in the shop file.

Showcase

Next🏆Example: Daily Rewards

🛍️Shops

🌱Example: Stock (like in life)

This means real life stock, for set limit of buy/sell times, please view Products page.

UltimateShop does not store a separate warehouse inventory. Instead, use server-wide transaction counts: the number sold becomes the buy limit.

Copy

Player A sells 5 items
↓
server sell count becomes 5
↓
shop buy limit becomes 5
↓
Player B can buy at most those 5 items

Set dynamic value for your product configs

The plugin itself does not store stock data, however, the logic of stock is essentially that player A sells an item to the shop, then the shop can sell the item sold by player A to player B. You only need to set a buy limit, and the value of the buy limit is the number of times the product is sold globally, that is, the placeholder: {server-times-server}. Read dynamic prices before read this page. Similar to Dynamic Prices, if you want to make stock system, do it in buy-limits option and put {server-times-server} placeholder in it, for example:

Copy

A:
price-mode: ALL
product-mode: CLASSIC_ALL
products:
1:
material: echo_shard
amount: 1
buy-prices:
1:
economy-plugin: EcoBits
economy-type: quest_points
amount: 5
placeholder: '&b{amount} Quest Points'
start-apply: 0
sell-prices:
1:
economy-plugin: EcoBits
economy-type: quest_points
amount: 5
placeholder: '&b{amount} Quest Points'
start-apply: 0
buy-limits:
global: '{sell-times-server}'
buy-times-reset-mode: 'NEVER'
buy-times-reset-time: '00:00:00'

We changed:

price-mode option to ANY or ALL.

buy-limits option to {sell-times-server} . For sell limits, you need write {buy-times-server} here. Replace the placeholder to {buy-times-player} and {sell-times-player} to make the stock be per player.

buy-limits-reset-mode option to 'NEVER'

In this way, we can ensure that players can only purchase items in the same quantity as the sell times (means restocking by other players sell it)

Variations

Replace server placeholders with player placeholders for a personal stock system.

Use {buy-times-server} in a sell limit to model demand before supply.

Add a fixed amount in a math expression to provide initial stock.

Combine this with dynamic prices so scarce products become more expensive.

Restock Manually

Restocking means changing transaction counts or the formula that determines the limit.

This content of auto reset buy/sell times is introduced on this page.

Use command /shop setselltimes <shop> <product> global <newStockPlusNowSellTimes>. If you want to restock 50 amount, and the now sell time is 75, then you need put 125 to <newStockPlusNowSellTimes>.

Previous⚡Example: Common Usage

Next📅Example: Daily Shop/Rotating Shop

Last updated 4 months ago

Set dynamic value for your product configs

Variations

Restock Manually

💹Price Modifier

This feature is offered to users as an early experimental feature and will be officially available after version 5.0.0.

The feature is still under testing and may change before its final release.

Price Modifier allows UltimateShop to adjust a product's sell reward according to properties of the actual item being sold.

For example, you can:

Reduce the sell price of a damaged item.

Read a numeric value from item lore and use it as the sell price.

Read a numeric value from NBT and use it as the sell price.

Apply different multipliers according to MythicChanger match-item rules.

Price modifiers are configured globally under:

Copy

sell:
price-modifier:

However, a product will only use them when the following option is enabled in that product:

Copy

price-modifier: true

See Products for more information about product options.

Item Matching vs Price Modifier

Item matching and Price Modifier are two different systems.

Item matching decides:

Can this item be sold as this product?

Price Modifier decides:

How much should this actual item be worth?

The simplified selling process is:

This distinction is especially important when a Price Modifier uses item data that is expected to change between different items.

For example, suppose a shop sells and buys a diamond sword.

The configured product contains:

But the player tries to sell:

If the two items fail the sell matching check, the damaged sword will never reach the durability Price Modifier.

The Price Modifier does not automatically make differently damaged, named, enchanted, or otherwise modified items match the same product.

Bukkit and ItemFormat sell methods

UltimateShop supports two global item matching methods:

and:

With Bukkit, UltimateShop uses Bukkit's item similarity check. Differences in item data such as durability, lore, enchantments, names, components, or NBT may cause an item to no longer match the configured product.

ItemFormat provides more control because specific item properties can be ignored during the matching process.

Example:

In this example, different durability values do not prevent items from matching the product.

Ignoring a property in item-format.ignore-key only affects item matching.

It does not remove that information from the actual item.

For example, if damage is ignored during matching, the durability Price Modifier can still read the actual damage value of the item being sold.

For more information about item matching, see Custom Item Match Method.

You can also hold an item and use:

to inspect its ItemFormat data and determine the correct key path.

Basic Configuration

A complete example can look like this:

Modifiers are processed in configuration order.

Enable Price Modifier for a Product

Price Modifier is disabled for products by default.

Enable it by adding:

to the product configuration.

Example:

The configured sell price is still the product's base sell price.

Price Modifier changes that price according to the actual item being sold.

Durability Modifier

The durability modifier reduces the sell price according to how much durability the item has lost.

Example:

The basic formula is:

For example:

If the base sell price is:

the modified price becomes:

Non-damageable items use a multiplier of 1.

minimum-multiplier

You can prevent damaged items from becoming almost worthless:

For example, even if the durability calculation produces:

the final modifier will be:

minimum-price

You can also specify a minimum numeric price:

This prevents the calculated numeric price from dropping below the configured value.

minimum-price and minimum-multiplier can be used separately or together.

Item matching for durability

If damaged and undamaged items should be treated as the same sellable product, their different damage values must not prevent item matching.

The recommended configuration is:

This creates the following behavior:

damage is ignored only when deciding whether the item matches the product. The modifier still receives the actual damaged ItemStack.

Lore Value Modifier

The Lore modifier reads a numeric value from an item's lore.

Example item:

Configuration:

The first matching lore line supplies the numeric value.

SET

With:

the captured value becomes the price of each sold trade unit.

For example:

results in:

When multiple trade units are sold together, UltimateShop keeps the result consistent with selling those units separately.

MULTIPLY

With:

the captured value multiplies the current price.

For example:

results in:

Numeric safety options

The following options prevent invalid or unreasonable values from being used:

Values outside the configured range are ignored.

Values longer than maximum-number-length are also ignored.

Missing, invalid, or unmatched values leave the current price unchanged.

Unsupported operations are reported as configuration errors and the affected modifier is ignored.

Item matching for Lore

If the lore used by the modifier is expected to differ between individual items, the lore difference must not prevent the item from matching the product.

For example:

If both should be sellable as the same product, you can use:

Ignoring lore during item matching does not prevent the Lore Price Modifier from reading the actual lore of the sold item.

However, ignoring the complete lore property means all lore differences are ignored during sell matching.

Only do this if all such lore differences are acceptable for that product.

For more precise item matching, consider using product-level match-item rules.

NBT Value Modifier

The NBT modifier reads a numeric value from the item's NBT data.

This modifier requires the NBTAPI plugin.

Example:

Nested NBT paths are supported:

value-type

Supported values are:

AUTO automatically attempts to obtain a usable numeric value.

Numeric strings are also accepted.

SET and MULTIPLY

The NBT modifier supports the same operations as the Lore modifier.

SET

uses the NBT value as the unit price.

MULTIPLY

multiplies the current price by the NBT value.

Invalid values

The modifier leaves the current price unchanged when the NBT value is:

Missing.

Invalid.

Non-numeric.

Non-finite.

Outside minimum-value and maximum-value.

Longer than maximum-number-length.

Item matching for NBT

If the NBT value used as the dynamic price differs between individual items, that value may also need to be ignored during ItemFormat matching.

For example, after inspecting the item with:

you may find a key such as:

You can then configure:

Whenever possible, ignore only the specific dynamic NBT key.

Avoid ignoring the entire nbt section unless you intentionally want all NBT differences to be ignored.

For example:

may cause many otherwise different items to be treated as the same product.

Use /shop generateitemformat to determine the most specific ItemFormat key available for your item.

Match Item Modifier

The match_item modifier uses MythicChanger match-item rules.

This modifier requires MythicChanger.

If MythicChanger is unavailable, the modifier has no effect.

Example:

Each rule contains:

The match-item section uses MythicChanger's item matching system.

MULTIPLY

produces a multiplier of:

which means:

of the current price.

ADD

adds the value to the multiplier.

For example:

which means:

of the current price.

A valid expression whose result is 0 is still accepted.

Invalid values or unsupported operations are reported through UltimateShop's error manager and the affected rule is ignored.

Multiple Match Item Rules

The mode option controls what happens when multiple rules match.

Supported modes are:

MAX / HIGHEST

Uses the highest resulting multiplier.

For example:

result:

MIN / LOWEST

Uses the lowest resulting multiplier.

For example:

result:

STACK

Processes matching rules in configuration order.

MULTIPLY values multiply the accumulated multiplier.

ADD values add to the accumulated multiplier.

For example:

Final multiplier:

Multiple Price Modifiers

All enabled Price Modifiers under:

are applied in configuration order.

For example:

the durability modifier is processed before the lore modifier.

The result of an earlier modifier becomes part of the current price used by later modifiers.

Therefore, changing the configuration order may also change the final result.

Selling Flow

Products with:

require access to the actual ItemStack being sold.

Because of this, sell flows that cannot provide an actual item stack do not directly display or execute the normal sell price.

When the player chooses:

or:

UltimateShop opens the item sell menu configured here:

Buy actions, amount-selection actions, and custom actions are not redirected by this behavior.

Bedrock forms and dialog shop lists open the product information view first, where buy and sell remain separate actions.

A product with Price Modifier enabled can still be sold through the item sell menu even if:

is configured for that product.

Price Calculation Order

The simplified numeric calculation is:

For example:

Precision and Rounding

Applying a multiplier is treated as a mathematical calculation.

UltimateShop rounds calculation results using:

to the decimal places configured by:

This applies to:

Price Modifiers.

Global sell multipliers.

Price previews.

Final sell rewards.

For more information, see Math Calculate Format.

Transaction Event Behavior

During an actual transaction, the sell price stored in the post-event GiveResult is authoritative.

If another plugin changes the sell price through:

UltimateShop preserves that changed result.

When different actual ItemStacks need different Price Modifier results, recalculated stack prices are used only to determine how the already-authoritative total should be distributed between those stacks before their item-aware modifiers are applied.

This prevents a transaction event's price modification from being accidentally replaced by a second normal price calculation.

Common Problems

Damaged items cannot be sold

If you are using:

but damaged items cannot be sold, check your global sell method.

Using:

may cause different damage values to fail item matching.

Consider:

with:

Lore modifier does not run

If the item's lore differs from the product item, the item may be rejected before the modifier is reached.

If that lore difference is intentional, configure the item matching system accordingly.

NBT modifier does not run

Check both systems separately:

Can the actual item match the product?

Can the NBT modifier find the configured NBT key?

Use:

to inspect ItemFormat matching data.

The NBT key used by the Price Modifier and the ItemFormat key used by ignore-key represent related item data, but they are configured for different purposes.

Price Modifier has no effect

Check that the product contains:

Also check that the corresponding global modifier contains:

For example:

Different items are unexpectedly treated as the same product

Check:

Do not ignore more item data than necessary.

For example:

is much broader than:

Use the most specific key possible.

Price Modifier API

Global item-aware Price Modifiers are managed by PriceModifierManager in cn.superiormc.ultimateshop.managers. It loads sell.price-modifier through the existing ConfigManager and rebuilds the configured chain on plugin reload. Custom factories remain registered across /shop reload.

A custom implementation only needs to implement PriceModifier and register a factory:

Register after UltimateShop has enabled, using a unique type ID. As with the action manager, registering an existing ID leaves the existing type unchanged. Registering a new type rebuilds the configured chain, so configured entries using that type become available immediately.

Each modifier receives:

The player.

The actual sold ItemStack.

The current numeric price.

It returns a non-negative multiplier.

The four-argument overload also receives the number of trade units for modifiers that require quantity-aware behavior, such as SET.

Modifiers configured under:

are applied in configuration order.

Registering a new factory, or replacing an existing factory with the same type, immediately rebuilds the configured Price Modifier chain.

A server reload is not required.

# Item matching method.
#
# ItemFormat is recommended when a Price Modifier depends on item
# properties that are expected to differ between otherwise sellable items.
sell-method: ItemFormat

item-format:
require-same-key: false
ignore-key:
# Allow different durability values to match the same product.
- 'damage'

# Global Price Modifier configuration.
price-modifier:

item-sell-menu:
# Products with `price-modifier: true` open this menu when
# the player chooses a sell action.
enabled: true
menu: item-sell

durability:
type: durability
enabled: true

# Losing 1% durability deducts 1% from the price.
deduction-coefficient: 1

# Final modifier cannot become lower than this value.
minimum-multiplier: 0.1

# You can also set a minimum numeric price.
# minimum-price: 1

lore:
type: lore
enabled: false

# SET or MULTIPLY.
operation: SET

# Example:
# Item Value: 500
pattern: 'Item Value[：:]\s*([+-]?(?:\d+(?:\.\d+)?|\.\d+))'

value-group: 1
strip-color: true
case-sensitive: true

minimum-value: 0
maximum-value: 1000000
maximum-number-length: 64

nbt:
type: nbt
enabled: false

# Requires NBTAPI.
key: item_value

# AUTO, BYTE, SHORT, INT, LONG, FLOAT, DOUBLE or STRING.
value-type: AUTO

# SET or MULTIPLY.
operation: SET

minimum-value: 0
maximum-value: 1000000
maximum-number-length: 64

mythic-changer:
type: match_item
enabled: false

# MAX, MIN or STACK.
mode: STACK

rules:
named-item:
operation: MULTIPLY
value: 1.2

match-item:
has-name: true

special-lore:
operation: ADD
value: 0.1

match-item:
contains-lore:
- 'Special'

🛍️Shops

♻️Product Config: Buy/Sell Times Reset

Reset

We will only attempt to reset under the following circumstances:

If the player is not on the server, the buy/sell times will only be reset after they join the server.

If the player is on the server, the buy/sell times will only be reset:

Before the player performs a purchase/sell operation

When the player opens the shop GUI

Auto reset (require enable use-times.auto-reset-mode option in config.yml file, will cost more server performance)

Reset require the products must has generated a reset time, if there is no reset time existed, it will naturally not be reset:

For reset mode that start with COOLDOWN_: require first view the product to generate new reset time, like open shop GUI).

For reset mode that NOT start with COOLDOWN_: require first buy or sell the product to generate new reset time.

These measures are aimed at optimizing the performance of plugins when resetting data, and we will not change these behaviors. If you are surprised by these behaviors and do not want to do so, then replacing with other plugins is a better choice.

Option Types

Buy times have those options:

buy-times-reset-mode (before 3.3.0 is buy-limits-reset-mode, but they are same)

buy-times-reset-time (before 3.3.0 is buy-limits-reset-time, but they are same)

buy-times-reset-time-format

buy-times-reset-value

buy-times-max-value

Sell times have those options:

sell-times-reset-mode (before 3.3.0 is sell-limits-reset-mode, but they are same)

sell-times-reset-time (before 3.3.0 is sell-limits-reset-time, but they are same)

sell-times-reset-time-format

sell-times-reset-value

sell-times-max-value

If you want to enable buy times and sell times reset for all products, you can simply modify it at config.yml file.

set-reset-value-by-default and max-value-for-total-only only exist in config.yml file.

No matter what methods you set it up in, we can see that this feature consists of 5 option types:

reset mode

reset time

reset time format (only required for CUSTOM type)

reset value

max value

Reset Mode

Support those modes:

NEVER:

TIMER: It will reset after the time you specify, for example, after 5 hours.

TIMED: It will be reset at the corresponding time, such as 8:15 pm.

RANDOM_PLACEHOLDER: Synchronize with the reset time of the specified random placeholder. (Added in 3.3.0) - Premium

CUSTOM: Directly enter the reset time in reset time, and the plugin will not perform any calculations. Recommend obtain reset time through the Placeholder API results. You need set time format at reset-time-format type option to helps us know how does your PlaceholderAPI results be like. (Added in 3.3.0) - Premium

COOLDOWN_TIMER (Added in 3.3.0)

COOLDOWN_TIMED (Added in 3.3.0)

COOLDOWN_CUSTOM (Added in 3.9.1) - Premium

Difference between COOLDOWN_TIMED (or COOLDOWN_TIMER) and TIMED (or TIMER)

TIMED and TIMER will start generating reset time after each buy or sell until the player reaches the limit and not save the generated reset time, while COOLDOWN_TIMED and COOLDOWN_TIMER will immediately start generating the next reset time after the last reset or first view this product (like open shop GUI) and save the generated time. It will not reset again unless the rest time is reached.

For this reason, when using COOLDOWN_TIMED or COOLDOWN_TIMER mode, the reset time will not automatically adjust due to server restarts, configuration modifications, or other reasons. This means that if you mistakenly set the product to refresh after 1 year, the reset time will not automatically change due to your correction, but TIMED or TIMER rules can do this.

Also, when use the last reset placeholders, COOLDOWN_TIMED or COOLDOWN_TIMER mode will return actual reset time, TIMED or TIMER mode will return first buy or sell time after last reset.

Reset Time

Different reset modes require different values to be filled in here. Supports placeholders, the placeholder used here must be on the server side, which means that all players receive the same value.

When using a reset mode that does not start with COOLDOWN_, please be careful about using dynamic placeholders in the reset time.

These modes do not store the timestamp of the next scheduled reset. Instead, their logic is to check whether the current time has surpassed the configured reset time whenever a purchase or sell occurs. If your reset time placeholder updates automatically before the transaction takes place, you may encounter a situation where the displayed reset time has updated, but the actual buy/sell counts remain un-reset.

If you don't understand this, there is a simplest rule of thumb: If you must use placeholders in your reset time, switch to a reset mode that starts with COOLDOWN_.

NEVER

Don't need anything here.

TIMER/COOLDOWN_TIMER

You can enter 3 to 5 numbers here, separated by a : symbol between each number. For example: 15:00:00.

Each number from right to left represents:

Seconds

Minutes

Hours

Days - Premium

Months - Premium

In this example, represents 15 hours later. Which means: if now time is 2023-09-04 12:00:00. Will reset after 15 hours, which means 2023-09-05 03:00:00.

TIMED/COOLDOWN_TIMED

The composition of TIMED and TIMER is almost identical, but the first three digits from the right-hand side represent the time of day. Let's also take 15:00:00 as an example:

If now time is 2023-09-04 12:00:00, will reset at 2023-09-04 15:00:00.

This is the result obtained with days set to 0. If you set it to 1, we will add another day, and that's it.

If you want to do a daily shop, days should be set to 0, and if you want to do a weekly shop, days should be set to 6. Because you need to reset the number of times on the last day, not on the second day after the last day, right?

This type of reset mode also supports set multi reset time, each reset time use ;; to splite, we will pick up the earliest reset time. For example: (Premium only)

In this example, this product or random placeholder will reset reset at 19:00 and 20:00 every day.

CUSTOM/COOLDOWN_CUSTOM - Premium

You only need to enter a Placeholder API placeholder here, and the result of the placeholder must include the complete year, month, day, hour, minute, and second. You also need to enter their format in the reset time format option, because different types of placeholders return different time formats, making it difficult for plugins to achieve uniformity.

RANDOM_PLACEHOLDER - Premium

Enter a valid random placeholder ID here.

Reset Value - Premium

By default, the reset value is 0, but, if you want to make some difference, this is allowed. Also this option supports placeholders, If combined with a random placeholder, it can achieve different reset values for players after each reset.

The reset value is apply for each reset, if player never buy or sell the product, you need consider set default value before using reset value. For more info, please view below "Default Value" section.

This option is very dangerous, you must set it carefully. You MUST ensure that the value of buy/sell limits is always greater than the value of buy/sell reset. This means that once reset, players must be able to repurchase/sell items again, otherwise you will see a situation where this item can never be purchased/sell again, and this situation is irreversible UNLESS you remove the buy/sell limits for this product or use commands to set the buy/sell times manually, REMEMBER THIS!

Default Value - Premium

The reset value will only be used after the buy times or sell times have been reset. Sometimes, if you not only want to do this, but also want to set it as the default value, you can enable the use-times.set-reset-value-by-default option in config.yml to do so. If you want a feature like default stock, it would be very useful.

Max Value - Premium

You can set maximum values for buy times and sell times, and when the maximum value is reached, the plugin will no longer accumulate buy times and sell times.

Please note:

After reaching the limit, players can still continue to purchase or sell products, but the plugin will not accumulate more times. If you want players to no longer purchase or sell products, then you should use the buy-limits option or sell-limits option in Products config instead of this feature.

Due to the setting of an upper limit that no longer accumulates, if the set upper limit is greater than the limit value, buy limits and sell limits will no longer be useful. Other features not mentioned may also be affected.

You can modify the use-times.max-value-for-total-only option in config.yml to ensure that the times placeholder accumulates normally even after reaching its maximum value, but the total placeholder does not continue to accumulate after reaching its maximum value. This can solve the problem told above. For information about these two placeholders, please refer to this page.

Dynamic Reset Time - Premium

This example uses a random placeholder to randomly refresh products after 3, 4, or 5 hours, instead of a fixed time refresh.

Created a random placeholder like this in random_placeholder folder:

Use this placeholder at buy-times-reset-time option in any product configs.

Dynamic Reset Value - Premium

By default, each reset will lead to player's buy times or sell times to 0, but you can also change it to different value, and even the random value!

Created a random placeholder config called resetvalue.yml in placeholders/random_placeholders folder:

Use this placeholder at buy-times-reset-value option in any product configs.

Cron Reset (Weekly Reset/Monthly Reset) - Premium

You can use Cron format in reset time.

Set reset mode to COOLDOWN_CUSTOM.

Use {cron_"<Cron Expression>"} built-in placeholder in reset time. Don't miss out the " symbol. The <Cron Expression> should use Quartz format.

For example:

Product config:

You can obtain the Cron expression you want by asking ChatGPT or use this tool. For example, the Cron expression in this example means to reset at 0:00 every Thursday. We do not provide any help related to how to write Cron expression.

You MUST make sure that time format of the result of Cron placeholder (set it in config.yml file) and the time format you set here is same. By default, they are same.

Reset Time does not correct?

The product must have been purchased or sold (for TIMED/TIMER) OR been viewed (for COOLDOWN_TIMED/COOLDOWN_TIMER) once before the next reset time can be stored. Otherwise, we can only display the possible reset time calculated based on the current time after the transaction is completed.

We will only reset the player's data when they are online. If the player is not online but has reached the reset time, we will reset it when they join the server again. The new reset time will be based on the current time, not the ideal reset time (because the player is not online at this time). The server data does not have this problem (because the server is always online), so it is very normal to use different reset times for the server and player. (Usually happens in COOLDOWN_TIMER reset mode)

Reset Time Updated but Buy times/Sell times not cleared?

If you must use placeholders in your reset time, switch to a reset mode that starts with COOLDOWN_.

Copy

use-times:
default-reset-mode: 'NEVER'
default-reset-time: '00:00:00'
# This only works for CUSTOM type of reset mode.
default-reset-time-format: 'yyyy-MM-dd HH:mm:ss'
default-reset-value: 0
# Set -1 to disable.
default-max-value: -1
# If set to true, product default buy / sell times will be set to reset value set in product configs or default value above.
set-reset-value-by-default: true
# If set to true, max value set in product configs or default value set above will only work for total placeholder.
max-value-for-total-only: true

Copy

sell-times-reset-mode: 'TIMED'
sell-times-reset-time: '20:00:00;;19:00:00'

Copy

reset-mode: ONCE
elements:
- '03:00:00'
- '04:00:00'
- '05:00:00'

Copy

B:
price-mode: ALL
product-mode: CLASSIC_ALL
products:
1:
material: GOLD_INGOT
amount: 1
buy-prices:
#
sell-prices:
#
buy-limits:
default: '2'
buy-times-reset-mode: 'COOLDOWN_TIMED'
buy-times-reset-time: '{random_reset}' # <--- Used here, sell-times also works!

Copy

reset-mode: ONCE
elements:
- '0~20' # A random number from 0 to 20
- '40' # A fixed number

Copy

B:
price-mode: ALL
product-mode: CLASSIC_ALL
products:
1:
material: GOLD_INGOT
amount: 1
buy-prices:
#
sell-prices:
#
buy-limits:
default: '2'
buy-times-reset-mode: 'TIMED'
buy-times-reset-time: '19:00:00;;20:00:00' # <--- TIMED mode supports multi reset time!
buy-times-reset-value: '{random_resetvalue}' # <--- Used random placeholder

Copy

sell-times-reset-mode: 'COOLDOWN_CUSTOM'
sell-times-reset-time: '{cron_"0 0 0 ? * 5"}'
# sell-times-reset-time-format: 'yyyy-MM-dd HH:mm:ss'
# You do not need set a time format here, this just help you know there is a option that you can set custom time format.

items:

display-name: "Apple"
price-mode: ANY
product-mode: ALL
products:
1:
material: APPLE
amount: 1
2:
material: BREAD
amount: 5

1:
type: permission

1:
economy-plugin: Vault
amount: 200
placeholder: '{amount} Coins'
start-apply: 0
2:

amount: 10
placeholder: '{amount} Points'
start-apply: 5

1:
economy-plugin: Vault
amount: 50
placeholder: '{amount} Coins'
2:
economy-plugin: PlayerPoints
amount: 1
start-apply: 5
placeholder: '{amount} Points'
give-actions:
1:
type: message:
message: 'Wow, seems that you have already sell 5 apples!'
buy-actions:
1:
type: player_command
command: 'say %player_name% purchased an Apple!'
2:
type: announcement
message: '&7%player_name% purchased an Apple!'
B:

material: BREAD
name: '&cSuper Bread'
display-name: "Bread"

- '@a&ePurchase: {buy-price}'
- '@b&eSell: {sell-price}'
- '&eDrop to buy, right to sell'

sell: 'RIGHT'

1:
